## dotgg's NIKKE data feed stopped updating (~2026-05); weapon stats for units after the cutoff are backfilled with manual stub files, not scraped
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: dotgg (`api.dotgg.gg`) stopped updating its NIKKE data around 2026-05 (confirmed by diffing its live character list against all 190 live characters). Units released after that cutoff (`ark-ranger-black`, `cinderella-crystal-wave`, `marciana-marine-study`, `prika`) have no dotgg source for weapon stats (`chargeTime` etc.) — lootandwaifus, the primary data source, only reports weapon TYPE, not the numeric stats the engine's weapon-stats loader needs.
- Decision: Fill dotgg-less units' weapon stats via hand-authored files, not a new scraper. `data/dotgg/char_<slug>.json` is created in dotgg's own schema, marked `"source": "manual"`. `scripts/collect_dotgg_weapons.py --stub <slug>` generates the template with a `_todo` list of the fields Fienn must fill in (5 fields for charge weapons, 3 for magazine weapons); Fienn deletes `_todo` once done.
- Why: New units land at roughly 1-2/month, so the manual burden is small. The alternative — scraping a third source (e.g. namuwiki) — was rejected as YAGNI: building and maintaining a parser costs more than occasionally hand-typing a handful of numbers.
- Consequences: Zero engine/loader changes — the existing weapon-stats field-presence check (`backend/app/user_roster.py::_weapon_stats`) is reused as-is (see the insight below on why unfilled fields must be omitted, not blanked). The collect-nikke workflow gains one more step after data collection (run the stub script, fill it in). If manual entries accumulate to the point of being a real burden, revisit and design a scraping source then.

## Summon/stack cadences come from measurement, not from the skill text
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: Encoding Ein and Raven, the skill text was actively misleading twice. Ein's Feather Shot reads "Activates when Near Feather is summoned ... Deals 90.81%", which parses as one hit per summon — but feathers persist and keep attacking, and their rate depends on how many are alive (Fienn, from a client datamine: 6 max, per-feather lifetimes, burst re-summons all six and resets their cooldowns, 8s attack cooldown at -16% per feather alive). Raven's Shock Wave reads "68.46% every 1 sec, stacks up to 10 times and lasts for 5 sec", which parses as an independent 5s DoT per Full Charge — but it is ONE counter, +1 per Full Charge, whose 5s life is refreshed by each Full Charge (Fienn).
- Decision: treat any summon-population or stack-cadence mechanic as unencodable from the text alone — ask Fienn, and where a formula and a measurement disagree, prefer the measurement and name the assumption in the module docstring. Concretely: Ein's feather hits use the additive -16% formula with a 0.3s floor between hits, calibrated to Fienn's video (FB entry +0.8s, then 0.3s apart, 31 hits); Raven's counter uses refresh semantics.
- Why: the errors were not marginal. Ein's literal reading gives 6 hits per Full Burst against 31 observed. Raven's literal reading settles at window/interval = 5 stacks where the real mechanic pins at the cap of 10 — her main damage source came out 1.9x too low (128M vs 292M over 180s) and shipped that way until Fienn caught it. Both are large enough to reorder deck recommendations. The multiplicative reading of Ein's -16% was rejected the same way: it predicts ~18 hits per Full Burst against 31 observed.
- Consequences: `scheduled_nukes` exists because of this (see below). Assumptions resting on a single observation are named as such — Ein's 0.3s throttle is verified only at 6 feathers; lower counts use the unverified formula. Both phrasings are recorded in the encoding skill's `special-mechanics.md`, since they will recur. Corollary, learned the hard way on Raven: do NOT reuse Velvet's "the cap can never bind, skip the resource" shortcut without checking the semantics — under refresh, the cap IS the steady state.

## New engine primitive `scheduled_nukes` for self-computed damage cadences
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: Ein's feather attacks and Raven's Shock Wave both land on a cadence `periodic_nukes` (a fixed interval) cannot express — Ein's varies with how many feathers are alive, Raven's with her own Full Charge times. Alternatives: approximate with a fixed-interval `periodic_nukes` at some average rate, or defer both units.
- Decision (Fienn, 2026-07-17): add `scheduled_nukes`, an opt-in `simulate_raid` param taking `{"schedule": fn(context, fight_duration) -> times, "percent", ...}`. The unit module computes the time list; the engine only emits it. Later the same day, extended so a schedule can read the owner's own firing timeline off `context.shot_times[slug]` (Raven's need) — the engine already kept `shot_times_by_slug`, so this exposed existing data rather than computing anything new, and left Ein's `(context, fight_duration)` signature untouched.
- Why: these schedules are fully deterministic — summons come from battle start plus the owner's burst times, both settled before the damage pass runs — so precomputing is exact, not an approximation. Keeping the computation in the unit module keeps summon-lifetime and stack bookkeeping out of the simulator, which would otherwise grow a concept per unit. A fixed-interval approximation was rejected: Ein's rate swings 30x between 1 and 6 feathers, so no single average is honest.
- Consequences: opt-in, so absent param = nothing emitted and no existing deck's numbers move. A schedule may repeat a time to emit one instance per stack (Raven), keeping defense subtracted per instance like a multi-hit burst. Reach for it only when the cadence genuinely varies — a fixed interval is still `periodic_nukes`. Catalogued in the encoding skill's `engine-capabilities.md`.

## Raven's part-destruction buff bracketed on `part_destructible`, not deferred
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: Raven's Blue Blade grants Single Point Attack (self Sustained Damage +47.32%/15s) when "an ally or self destroys an enemy's part". The engine has no concept of parts, so it cannot say when that happens. Alternatives: defer it (Sakura's three part-destruction riders were deferred exactly this way), or bracket it.
- Decision (Fienn, 2026-07-17): bracket it on the existing `BossProfile.part_destructible` flag, following Ark Ranger Black's precedent — floor (no destructible parts) never grants it; ceiling (destructible parts, and Raven's own Vital Attack exists to break them) treats it as up from battle start. Vital Attack itself stays deferred: Damage to Parts has no consumer in the engine, so wiring it would be inert.
- Why: the flag answers "can parts be destroyed at all", which is enough to bound the buff even though the timing is unknowable. Deferring would silently understate her on part-destructible bosses; assuming it always fires would overstate her everywhere else. The measured spread is small (floor 345.4M vs ceiling 351.1M, ~1.7%), so this is a narrow bracket, not a coin flip.
- Consequences: part destruction itself is still unmodeled — this brackets the answer, it does not compute it. A.N. Mode's "Removes Single Point Attack" is deliberately not modeled (it would cut the ceiling's own buff during her burst, and there is no evidence her rotation actually loses it there). Sakura's part riders remain deferred; all three would only ADD damage, so her encoding is a floor.

## Worktree data sync is automatic (script + SessionStart hook), not a remembered step
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: `data/dotgg/` and `data/lootandwaifus/` are gitignored, and git populates a worktree only from tracked files — so a worktree starts without them, or with a stale subset. A loadability probe run inside this worktree reported Ein as having no dotgg weapon file, and that was written into `docs/roadmap.md` and `docs/encoded-nikkes.md` as fact ("dotgg shut down, no honest source for her weapon stats"). Fienn caught it: the file was in main all along. The worktree was 18 files behind; syncing moved loadable from 50/58 to 54/58 (ein, ark-ranger-black, prika, marciana-marine-study were all "missing" for the same reason, and prika's return unblocked the mint+prika Encore synergy in real deck search).
- Decision (Fienn, 2026-07-17): sync automatically, never ask. `scripts/sync_worktree_data.py` (no-op in main, copies only missing files, never overwrites or deletes, `--check` reports drift and exits 1) plus a SessionStart hook in `.claude/settings.json`.
- Why: a one-liner `cp` depends on someone remembering, and the failure mode is silent — absent data reads as a real result and gets written into docs as fact. The hook lives in project settings rather than `settings.local.json` precisely because local settings are untracked and would not reach a worktree — the exact propagation gap being fixed. `|| true` so it never blocks session start, but stderr stays visible: a silent failure would put us back to measuring missing data as fact.
- Consequences: the hook takes effect from the next session, and reaches new worktrees only once this branch merges. The same trap applies to `node_modules` / `.venv`, which the script does not cover. Recorded in `docs/insights.md` under "Data".

## Sub-minute five-deck allocation (batched swap scoring / smaller swap budget) deferred until real user feedback
> **초과됨 (2026-07-25)** — 맨 위 "swap 힐클라임 배치 병렬화" 항목 참고. 레버 (a) 배치
> 채점은 채택, (b) 예산 축소는 기각. 이 결정의 근거였던 "swap = 전체의 5.0%"는 캐스케이드
> 이전 프로파일이었고, 재측정에서 swap이 품질의 주력(+48.6%)이면서 한 패스의 52%만 보고
> 잘린다는 것이 드러나 전제가 무효가 됐다.
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: `ProcessPool` parallelism (decision below) cut five-deck allocation from 282.17s (3 decks, 42 units, serial) to 97.25s (all 5 decks, 50 units, workers=auto — commit `0b48277`). The original target was "seconds to a minute"; 97.25s is close but still over. Profiling attributed the residual overrun to the swap hill-climb phase, which stays serial (each accepted swap changes the state the next candidate is judged against, so its iterations can't be trivially fanned out) and runs under its own ≤45s budget (see the swap-phase-budget entry below) — the dominant remaining cost. Two follow-up levers were identified to close the gap: (a) batch-evaluate swap candidates instead of judging them one at a time, or (b) shrink the swap phase's time budget outright.
- Decision (Fienn, 2026-07-17): defer both levers. Do not implement now; revisit if real user feedback indicates 97s is actually a problem in practice.
- Why: both levers have a real cost with no confirmed payoff yet — batched swap scoring is nontrivial implementation complexity (the serial-dependency problem above needs a real design, not just a parallel map), and shrinking the swap budget directly trades away search quality (fewer swap iterations found real improvements, per the swap-phase-budget entry below). Spending either cost now, before any user has complained that ~1-2 minutes is too slow, is exactly the kind of speculative work YAGNI argues against — especially since the frontend already sets the expectation: `RecommendPanel`'s raid-mode status message tells the user to expect "1–2 minutes" (`frontend/src/components/RecommendPanel.test.tsx`'s `/1–2 minutes/` assertion), so the current wall-clock cost is already inside the UX contract, not a surprise regression.
- Consequences: 97.25s stands as the shipped figure; no further allocation-speed work is planned unless triggered by user feedback. If revisited, the two candidate levers remain: batched swap-candidate evaluation (preserves search quality, higher implementation cost) or a smaller swap time budget (cheap to implement, trades away swap-phase quality — see the swap-phase-budget entry's rationale for why the swap phase is where quality actually comes from). See `docs/roadmap.md` Phase 5/6 status and `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-17-processpool-parallelism.md` for the parallelization implementation itself.

## Phase 5 next-work priority: manifest-exception unit backfill before parallelization/frontend wiring
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: With the ProcessPool throughput lever decided (entry below) and the 5-deck allocator otherwise tested against real data, two follow-on tracks were both open: (a) implementing `ProcessPool` simulation parallelism and wiring the frontend results UI to show multiple decks, vs. (b) backfilling the 8 remaining `KNOWN_MANIFEST_EXCEPTIONS` units (`crown`, `helm`, `liter`, `miranda`, `moran`, `soline-frost-ticket`, `volume`, `zwei`) whose dotgg-source skill-value manifests are still missing — their weapon-stat files are already collected, only the manifest wiring is left. The real-roster measurement (entry below) found only 3 of 5 target decks buildable because the loadable roster has just 4 Burst-1 units, too few to fill a 4th/5th deck under `ALLOWED_SHAPES`.
- Decision (Fienn, 2026-07-17): do the 8-unit manifest-exception batch first, then move to parallelization and frontend wiring.
- Why: "only 3 decks, not 5" is a roster-size limitation, not an algorithm-speed or UI limitation — running faster or displaying results more nicely doesn't fix a roster too thin in Burst-1 units to fill 5 decks. Most of the 8 exception units are Burst-1s (per `docs/roadmap.md`'s earlier batch grouping — `liter`, `miranda`, `moran`, `volume`, `zwei`, `soline-frost-ticket`), so loading them directly targets the actual blocker before spending effort on levers that can't move it.
- Consequences: `ProcessPool` implementation and frontend multi-deck wiring remain queued but deprioritized until the manifest batch lands and the resulting deck count is re-measured. See `docs/roadmap.md` Phase 6 for the up-to-date `KNOWN_MANIFEST_EXCEPTIONS` list and dotgg-source status.

## Throughput lever for the 282s five-deck allocation overrun: simulation parallelism (ProcessPool), not a smaller tier cap
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: The entry below measured the shipped peeling+swap algorithm at 282.17s against a seconds-to-a-minute budget target and left the throughput-lever choice open between two alternatives: (a) shrink the search's tier cap (trade search breadth/quality for speed), or (b) parallelize the simulation itself via `ProcessPool` (trade implementation complexity for speed at unchanged breadth).
- Decision (Fienn, 2026-07-17): `ProcessPool` simulation parallelism. Tier-cap reduction rejected.
- Why: tier-cap reduction directly degrades search quality (fewer candidates considered per deck — a real accuracy cost that compounds across 5 decks on a roster already B1-scarce, per the `ALLOWED_SHAPES` entry below) in exchange for speed. `ProcessPool` parallelism buys the same throughput without touching what's searched — a pure wall-clock win with no accuracy tradeoff, since `evaluate_deck` calls are independent and the peeling algorithm's separability (same-boss decks, entry below) means nothing about correctness depends on execution order.
- Consequences: Implementation is a follow-on task, not done as of this entry (see the priority-ordering entry above — it's queued behind the manifest-exception backfill). Parallelization is explicitly expected to absorb both the 282s allocation-level overrun AND the residual per-sim overhead the Stage 0.5 `evaluate_deck` optimization left on the table (103.43ms/sim, 2.1x over its own 50ms target — see that entry's follow-up note further below), rather than requiring a further per-call optimization pass on either front.

## Phase 5 five-deck allocation: real allocation is 282.17s (3 decks, 5.18B) against a seconds-to-a-minute budget
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: First full-roster measurement of `allocate_decks` against all 42 loadable units, taken after the swap-budget fix (below) and the Cinderella double-unwrap regression fix landed.
- Decision (recorded as status, not a choice): 42 loadable units → 3 decks built (peeling + swap ≤45s + polish), leftover 27 units, combined 5.18B total damage. Only 3 decks were possible, not 5, because the loadable roster has just 4 Burst-1 units — too few B1s remain after the first few decks to fill a 4th/5th deck under the `ALLOWED_SHAPES` constraint (below). `prika` cannot be loaded from the real roster at all (no dotgg weapon-stats file for her) — the mint+prika `SYNERGY_SETS` pairing only takes effect once she's loadable.
- Why: This is the actual measured cost of the shipped algorithm on real data, needed to decide whether the seconds-to-a-minute budget target is met (it is not — 282.17s is roughly 5-300x over depending on which target figure is used) and whether the fix is a smaller tier cap (trade search breadth for speed) or `ProcessPool` simulation parallelism (trade implementation complexity for speed at the same breadth).
- Consequences: The throughput-lever choice (tier-cap tuning vs. `ProcessPool` parallelization) was resolved to `ProcessPool` — see the dedicated entry above, which also explains why (accuracy-preserving). The 4-B1 scarcity means shape/allocation quality can't be fully evaluated until more B1 units are loadable — that gap is being closed first, ahead of the parallelization work itself (see the priority-ordering entry above) — or `prika`'s weapon data is collected. See `docs/roadmap.md` Phase 5 status for the up-to-date figure — an earlier 103.92s figure from the same day was a measurement bug (see the swap-budget entry below) and should not be cited as the algorithm's real cost.

## Swap phase gets its own time budget, anchored after (unbudgeted) peeling completes
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: The five-deck allocator (`backend/app/deck_allocation.py`) runs greedy peeling then a same-tier swap hill-climb, both under one nominal `time_budget_sec`. The deadline was originally computed once at entry (`start + time_budget_sec`), before peeling ran. Peeling itself is unbudgeted and took ~100s+ on the real 42-unit roster, so the shared deadline had already passed by the time peeling finished — the swap phase, which is where quality improvements actually come from, never ran a single iteration. The first full-roster measurement (103.92s, 3 decks) was silently swap-free because of this.
- Decision (Fienn): give the swap phase its own budget, always up to the full `time_budget_sec` measured from when peeling completes, not from function entry. Peeling is treated as an unbudgeted prerequisite step whose own cost isn't charged against the swap deadline.
- Why: the swap hill-climb is the actual quality mechanism (peeling alone is a fast but locally greedy first pass); anchoring its deadline before an unbudgeted, unpredictable-length peeling phase meant the phase that mattered most for quality was starved to zero on any roster large enough for peeling to take non-trivial time — exactly the case that matters (the real roster).
- Consequences: `time_budget_sec` now bounds two phases separately rather than one shared window (`docs/roadmap.md` and `f31fc32`'s note both updated to say the budget caps only the swap phase). Re-measuring with this fix gave the real 282.17s/3-deck/5.18B figure above — noticeably slower than the old (buggy) 103.92s, but that number represented incomplete work, not a faster algorithm.

## Synergy candidate-pool shells are measured over both intra-pair orderings, not the alphabetical order
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: `prune_candidate_pool`'s synergy-set marginal-score measurement (`backend/app/deck_search.py`) placed a `SYNERGY_SETS` pair into a reference shell in a fixed (alphabetical-slug) order. `burst_cycle` fires the leftmost eligible same-tier unit first — with the alphabetical order, Prika landed after Mint in the shell and never got to burst before her, so her Encore (which requires Mint to have already sung) never fired during the measurement. Prika and Mint's synergy score was silently near-zero. Caught in final review (Critical).
- Decision: measure a synergy pair's shell score under BOTH orderings of the pair (`members` and `members[::-1]`) and take the max, rather than a single fixed order. Applies to both `SYNERGY_SETS` entries: `{mint, prika}` and `{mast-romantic-maid, anchor-innocent-maid}`. Curation constants: `SYNERGY_SETS` (explicit pairs) plus `WEAPON_SYNERGY_ANCHORS = {"tove": "SG"}` (a themed pool — Tove pulls in SG-weapon B3 attackers as candidates whenever she's rostered, since a plain marginal-score cut would otherwise miss units whose value depends on her weapon-scoped buffs rather than direct stacking).
- Why: `burst_cycle`'s left-priority firing order makes a synergy pair's measured value order-dependent whenever one member's payoff requires the other to act first (Prika's Encore is exactly this shape) — a single fixed order silently measures only one of the two real in-game orderings, understating (here, near-zeroing) the pair's true synergy value.
- Consequences: The same commit also fixed marginal scores to be baseline-subtracted (each reference pass computes its own baseline once; a candidate already present in the reference correctly scores 0.0 without a redundant re-simulation) — a correctness cleanup found during the same review pass, not a new modeling choice. Any future `SYNERGY_SETS` addition must be checked for the same left-priority ordering trap before trusting a single-order measurement. See `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-17-five-deck-allocation.md`'s "Amendments (final review, 2026-07-17)" section.

## Prika's Encore self burst-cooldown +21s is now encoded (reverses prior "rotation bookkeeping, not DPS" deferral)
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: Prika's Encore effect 4 ("Cooldown of Burst Skill ▲21 sec, self") was previously filed as not-modeled — reasoned as pure rotation bookkeeping with no squad-DPS effect, since her Charge Damage refresh already captured the outcome of Performance staying up. Building the five-deck allocator's real burst-cycle simulation exposed that this was wrong for the SIMULATOR specifically: without the cooldown extension, Prika re-bursts every ~40s in the sim and displaces Mint out of the tier-2 burst slot, distorting the rotation away from Fienn's intended pattern (Mint owns the tier-2 slot from cycle 2 onward).
- Decision: encode it as a negative `burst_cooldown_reduction_sec` value (`-encore_cd_increase`) pushed through a `Pulse` on the existing burst-CDR-reduction path, self-scoped, fired from `apply_burst` alongside Encore's other effects (`backend/app/skill_rules/prika.py`). No engine change — reuses the existing (positive-valued) burst-CDR pulse mechanism with a negative number.
- Why: a mechanic being "not squad-DPS" doesn't make it inert in a simulator that actually schedules burst timing — leaving it out let a wrong rotation (Prika re-bursting instead of Mint) silently distort every deck-search result involving her, which only became visible once the search started actually exercising multi-cycle burst rotation at scale.
- Consequences: `prika.py`'s module docstring and "Not modeled" list updated to drop this item (only Performance-duration +21s remains bookkeeping-only, unmodeled). Any other unit whose kit was previously deferred as "rotation bookkeeping, no DPS effect" should be re-checked against how burst-slot ownership actually plays out under multi-cycle simulation, not assumed inert by the same reasoning that turned out wrong here.

## Deck-shape search is hard-constrained to (1,1,3)/(1,2,2)/(2,1,2), not "at least one of every tier"
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: The five-deck allocator's underlying single-deck search (`shape_combinations`, `backend/app/deck_search.py`) needed a tier-shape rule for which 5-unit Burst-1/2/3 combinations are worth generating. The general engine constraint up to this point was just "at least one of each tier" — which also admits shapes real play never uses (e.g. (3,1,1), (1,3,1)).
- Decision (Fienn): restrict shape enumeration to exactly three tuples of (B1, B2, B3) counts — `(1,1,3)`, `(1,2,2)`, `(2,1,2)` — reflecting Fienn's domain knowledge of which shapes real deck-building actually uses, rather than every combinatorially valid "at least one per tier" shape.
- Why: the untargeted "at least one per tier" rule was structurally correct but wasteful and meta-inaccurate — it would spend search budget generating and scoring shapes no real player deck takes, and could surface a "best" deck in a shape nobody would actually build. Hard-coding the three real shapes shrinks the search space to what matters and keeps recommendations aligned with how decks are actually played.
- Consequences: `ALLOWED_SHAPES = ((1, 1, 3), (1, 2, 2), (2, 1, 2))` in `deck_search.py` is now the sole shape filter for `shape_combinations`; any future shape found to be real play (a 4th tuple) needs an explicit addition here, not a reversion to the general rule. This constraint is also why the five-deck allocator's real-roster run could only build 3 decks (see the allocation-measurement entry above) — a roster with only 4 loadable B1 units runs out of B1 slots to fill these shapes well before 5 decks are built, and that scarcity is inherent to the roster, not a search-quality bug.

## Five-deck allocation algorithm: greedy peeling + same-tier swap hill-climb, not ILP or the search's own enumeration
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: Phase 5 needed to split a full roster into up to 5 non-overlapping decks maximizing combined damage against the same boss. Because every deck fights the SAME boss, total damage is separable — it's exactly the sum of each deck's own independently-computed score, which rules out needing a joint objective function and opens the door to a much simpler algorithm than a general multi-deck joint optimizer.
- Decision (Fienn): (1) build decks one at a time via greedy peeling — repeatedly take the best available deck from the remaining roster (via the existing budget-aware single-deck search) and remove its members before searching for the next deck; (2) follow with a same-tier swap hill-climb across the built decks, trying same-burst-tier unit swaps between decks to find local improvements, budgeted separately (see the swap-budget entry above). Alternatives considered and rejected: ILP/exact set-packing over all possible deck combinations (rejected — pre-computing every combination's score to feed an ILP solver was estimated at tens of hours on the real roster, infeasible); a two-stage "proxy score, then exact enumeration" scheme (rejected as the primary mechanism — the proxy's own enumeration step was itself the bottleneck being avoided, so it could only ever serve as a subordinate helper, not the main algorithm).
- Why: same-boss deck separability means a good deck for the "current best roster state" is very likely still a good deck once its members are removed — greedy peeling exploits this directly instead of paying for global joint optimization the objective's structure doesn't actually require. The swap hill-climb recovers most of what greedy peeling's local-only choices miss (a member that would be better used in a different deck) without the combinatorial cost of ILP.
- Consequences: `backend/app/deck_allocation.py`'s `allocate_decks` is peeling + swap, not exact — it is not guaranteed globally optimal, only a good-quality heuristic bounded by its time budget. This is the algorithm measured at 282.17s/3-decks/5.18B on the real roster (entry above). The swap phase needing its own budget (separate entry above) was a bug found after this algorithm shipped, not part of the original algorithm choice itself.

## Stage 0.5: epoch + stat-bundle memo cuts `total_for` call count, but 50ms target not reached — remainder handed to Fienn
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: After Stage 0's segment-table rewrite (below) got `evaluate_deck` to 133.66ms, profiling showed the remainder was flat per-call overhead: `total_for` was still being called ~255K times per sim (17 stats × per-damage-instance), each paying Python call/dict overhead even though the segment-table lookup itself is now cheap. Fienn's call was to attack call COUNT next, not per-call cost.
- Decision: Add `EffectRegistry.state_epoch(target, time)` — the union of every stat's boundary times for a target, collapsed into a single integer timeline — as a memo key, combined with `(slug, epoch, version)` to bundle all 17 phase-2-consumed stats (`_BUNDLE_STATS` in `raid_simulator.py`) into one dict resolved once per (target, epoch, version) instead of once per stat per call. Implemented as `raid_simulator.simulate_raid`'s local `_stat_bundle(slug, time)` closure, memoized in a plain dict keyed on `(slug, registry.state_epoch(target, time), registry.version)`.
- Why: Within one state epoch, every one of the 17 bundled stats is provably constant (see the epoch-superset-invariant insight below), so caching the whole bundle together — rather than each stat separately — is safe and cuts repeated `total_for` calls from 17/instance to amortized ~0/instance after the first hit in an epoch.
- Consequences: 133.66ms → 103.43ms (23% further cut on top of Stage 0's 94.5%). **The spec's 50ms target was NOT reached (2.1× over)** — the remaining cost is per-damage-event fixed overhead (dict construction + `calculate_damage` invoked ~21K times/sim), which no memo can remove since each call needs a genuinely distinct answer. Per the spec's own stop condition, no further mechanism was built without a doc-and-decide checkpoint — logged here and handed to Fienn to decide whether 103ms is acceptable or a different engine-level change (e.g. reducing damage-event count itself) is warranted. **Follow-up decision (Fienn, 2026-07-17): stop here.** 103.43ms is accepted as the final single-sim cost — the remaining gap is flat call overhead with no further algorithmic lever visible (ROI on more per-call optimization has collapsed), so the 2.1× shortfall against the 50ms target is absorbed by simulation-level parallelism instead (`ProcessPool`, see the throughput-lever decision above) rather than by a further per-call optimization pass. **`_stat_bundle` is phase-2-only** — see the insight below on the phase-1 version-churn trap this decision's implementation ran into and reverted from. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-effect-registry-performance-design.md`; implementation plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-17-effect-registry-stage05.md`.

## Stage 0: EffectRegistry rewritten to per-(stat, slug, element) segment tables + bisect, replacing the full linear Effect scan
- Date: 2026-07-17
- Context: `evaluate_deck` (the deck-search inner loop) took 2.4s/sim, 93% of it inside `EffectRegistry.total_for` — the original implementation linearly scanned every registered `Effect` on every call (5.25M active-effect checks per sim) to sum whichever ones matched a `(stat, target, time)` query. This made the simulator too slow to use for the planned larger deck search.
- Decision: Replace the linear scan with a per-`(stat, slug/scope-key, element)` segment table: each key maps to a sorted list of boundary times and running-sum segments, looked up via `bisect`. Invalidated by a registry-wide `version` counter bumped on every `add`/`add_refreshing`/`truncate_open_ended` call, so any mutation forces a full rebuild of affected segment tables rather than requiring incremental patching. **Hard constraint: bit-identical output** to the original scan — segment sums are accumulated in EFFECT INSERTION ORDER (not chronological order), because float addition is non-associative and re-ordering the sum by time could flip the last bit of a result versus the original code path. Verified via a parity net that runs the new segment-table implementation against a frozen, never-modernized copy of the original naive scan across the full existing test suite, plus dedicated segment-boundary/negative-window-truncation cases.
- Alternatives considered & rejected: (1) a stat-only index (drop the slug/element dimension) — too coarse, would still scan every effect matching just the stat, most of which don't match the actual target; (2) numpy-vectorized summation — pulls in a new dependency and requires a much larger rewrite for a problem this segment-table approach solves without one.
- Why: The `(stat, slug, element)` key is exactly the dimensionality `total_for`'s callers query on, so segment tables collapse what was an O(effects) scan into an O(log segments) bisect per call with no loss of query generality. Preserving insertion-order summation was non-negotiable per the parity requirement — this is a performance rewrite, not a numerics change, and any bit drift would be indistinguishable from a real bug during the parity net's own construction.
- Consequences: 2410ms → 133.66ms (94.5% reduction). The parity-net test pattern (frozen naive reference + cross-check) is a reusable template for future bit-identical performance rewrites (see the insight below). The existing test suite itself got 3× faster as a side effect (22s → 6.5s), since many tests exercise `total_for` heavily. Design spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-effect-registry-performance-design.md`.

## dotgg data files are indexed by their JSON "url" field, never by filename
- Date: 2026-07-16
- Context: Building the roster assembly layer's dotgg-source skill-value parser and weapon-stats loader (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-16-roster-assembly-api-design.md`). dotgg character JSON filenames use dotgg's OWN slug convention (e.g. `char_drake-nikke.json`), which does not match this project's roster slug (`drake`) — a naive `data/dotgg/char_<our_slug>.json` filename guess fails for any unit whose dotgg slug differs from ours.
- Decision: Locate a unit's dotgg data file by reading each file's own JSON `"url"` field (which encodes dotgg's slug unambiguously) and matching it against the unit, rather than constructing a filename from our slug. Implemented in `backend/app/skill_values.py` (`_dotgg_path_for`).
- Why: Filename-guessing silently fails (file not found → wrongly excluded) whenever the two slug conventions diverge, and that divergence is common enough (Drake is the first observed case) that it can't be dismissed as a one-off. Reading the file's own declared identity is robust to any slug-naming mismatch and doesn't require a hand-maintained our-slug→dotgg-slug lookup table.
- Consequences: Every dotgg-backed load (skill-value parsing source `"dotgg"`, and weapon_stats in `user_roster.py`) goes through `_dotgg_path_for` rather than a direct filename join. Adding a new dotgg-sourced unit needs no manual slug-mapping entry — it "just works" as long as the data file's `url` field is present, which is a data-collection precondition, not a code change.

## Skill-value manifests are colocated in each skill_rules module, verified by a fixture-comparison assembly harness
- Date: 2026-07-16
- Context: Roster assembly (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-16-roster-assembly-api-design.md`) needs to turn a user's per-skill-level data into the `skill_values` dict each unit's builders expect (keyed by module-chosen sub-skill names, e.g. `values["transform"]`, holding `description_value_NN` slots). No code previously mapped a unit's builder-facing key names to skill-level slot indexes — that mapping only existed implicitly in each encoder's head when they hand-transcribed test fixtures.
- Decision: Each `skill_rules/<slug>.py` module declares its own `SKILL_VALUE_MANIFESTS` dict next to the builders whose key names it describes (mirroring the existing `_BUILDERS`-style per-module registration pattern), aggregated by `registry.get_skill_value_manifest(slug)`. Correctness is anchored by an assembly harness (`backend/tests/test_skill_value_assembly.py`) that, for every unit with a manifest, parses max-level `skill_values` from the real local data files and compares each slot against that SAME unit's own hand-transcribed test fixtures (the existing `TRANSFORM`/`ULTIMATE`/... module-level constants used to test the builders) — the fixtures are treated as ground truth since they were verified during encoding. A known hazard (encoders sometimes skip a non-value number in the raw text, e.g. "Burst stage 3," while counting another, e.g. "after 100 normal attack(s)") is handled by a per-unit `drop_tokens` override inside its `SKILL_VALUE_MANIFESTS` entry added only where the harness actually fails — never a speculative heuristic. Alternative considered and rejected: a single central slug→manifest table in `registry.py` (rejected — it would live apart from the builders whose key-name conventions it encodes, and would drift out of sync as builders change their key names without a forcing function to update it).
- Why: Colocation keeps the mapping beside the code whose key-name choices it depends on, the same way `_BUILDERS` registration already works — a builder rename is caught immediately (or at least locally) rather than silently breaking a distant central table. Comparing against each unit's own existing test fixtures (rather than writing new expected values) reuses already-verified ground truth instead of introducing a second, unverified source of truth for the same numbers.
- Consequences: A module without a `SKILL_VALUE_MANIFESTS` entry is simply not loadable from user data — excluded and reported (see the exclusion-policy decision below), not an error. Mismatches must be fixed via per-unit `drop_tokens` overrides, never by loosening the harness's comparison or editing the fixtures themselves — the fixtures are load-bearing ground truth for both the builder tests and this harness. New encodings must declare a manifest and pass the harness as a normal step of the encoding workflow going forward (per the `nikke-skill-encoding` skill). First batch: `ade_agent_bunny`, `anchor_innocent_maid`, `arcana`, `arcana_fortune_mate`, `blanc`, `brid_silent_track`, `drake`, `grave`, `little_mermaid`, `mast_romantic_maid`, `mint`, `modernia`, `nayuta`, `rapi_red_hood`, `rei_ayanami` — the remaining encoded roster is a pending backfill batch.

## Non-loadable roster entries are excluded and reported, never a 422 by themselves
- Date: 2026-07-16
- Context: `POST /api/recommend` (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-16-roster-assembly-api-design.md`) takes a user's full roster, which will routinely include Nikkes this engine can't yet simulate: not encoded at all, encoded but with no `SKILL_VALUE_MANIFESTS` entry yet (see above), or missing dotgg weapon stats (lootandwaifus-only units whose weapon data hasn't been collected). The question was what the endpoint should do when some roster entries can't be turned into a `NikkeSpec`.
- Decision (Fienn, 2026-07-16): Users submit their whole roster as-is; any entry `load_nikke_spec` can't assemble is silently dropped from the search and its slug is listed in the response's `excluded_slugs` field — this by itself is never an error response. The endpoint only returns 422 when the REMAINING usable roster can't form any feasible deck (fewer than 5 usable units, or a burst tier missing after exclusions), and that 422's detail message names which slugs were excluded so the cause is diagnosable. Implemented in `backend/app/user_roster.py` (`load_roster` returns `(specs, excluded_slugs)`) and `backend/app/api.py`.
- Why: Forcing users to hand-curate their roster down to only fully-supported Nikkes before submitting would be poor UX and would leak engine-coverage internals into the request contract; the UI can label unsupported characters using `excluded_slugs` while still letting the search run on whatever the user does own that's usable. Reserving 422 for actual infeasibility (not "some units unsupported") keeps the error meaning precise: "your submission produced no deck," not "your roster wasn't 100% supported."
- Consequences: `excluded_slugs` is now a permanent, load-bearing part of the `/api/recommend` response contract (`frontend/README.md` updated to match) and the frontend results view surfaces it ("not yet supported") rather than treating any exclusion as a hard failure. Engine-coverage gaps (missing manifests, missing dotgg stats, unencoded units) degrade gracefully per-unit instead of blocking the whole request — consistent with the project's existing "exclude and report" posture rather than an all-or-nothing gate.
## Gap #3: member-subset targeting resolved as a live-filter "slugs:" scope, not a new Effect scope
- Date: 2026-07-16
- Context: Phase C's gap #3 covers buffs whose target set is a narrow, dynamic member subset that `Effect.scope`'s static options (`self`/`squad`/`element:X`) can't express — e.g. Ark Ranger Black's "all Wind Code allies with assault rifles," Arcana's "all Burst 3 Electric allies," and Ada Wong's "all Burst 3 allies who previously used their Burst Skill this cycle." The last one depends on live per-cycle state (`burst_used_this_cycle`), not just static member metadata.
- Decision: Resolve these at trigger time via a live filter `member_filter(member, context) -> bool`, encoded as the existing `"slugs:a,b"` scope — the same mechanism `top_atk_slugs`/`highest_atk_buff_rule` already established for "top-N highest ATK" targeting. One new helper, `member_subset_buff_rule` (`backend/app/skill_rules/_helpers.py`), covers both the static-metadata sub-kind (weapon/element filters) and the dynamic-state sub-kind (burst-used-this-cycle filters) — the filter callable is the only thing that varies. `SquadMember` (`squad_engine.py`) gained an optional `weapon: str | None = None` field, threaded from each roster deck dict's `.get("weapon")` (`raid_simulator.py`), so weapon-conditional filters have something to read; defaults to `None` for any caller not passing it, so tests/callers that don't need weapon targeting are unaffected.
- Alternatives considered & rejected: (1) a new static `weapon:<type>` Effect scope (would handle Ark Ranger's case but not Ada Wong's, since scope matching is inherently static — it can't express "who has already burst this cycle"); (2) a separate dynamic-subset scope mechanism alongside a static weapon scope (two mechanisms for what turns out to be the same shape — resolve-live-to-slugs — once Ada Wong's case showed the "static filter" and "dynamic filter" cases only differ in what the predicate reads, not in how the result is applied).
- Consequences: Filters run ONCE at trigger time — the target set is a snapshot of state at that instant, not continuously re-evaluated as the fight progresses (matches the existing `top_atk_slugs` precedent's same limitation). Consumers: Ark Ranger Black (Wind-AR ally Sustained Damage buff), Arcana: The Magician/Strength (bursted-Electric-B3 subset buffs), Tove (SG-ally Attack Speed + flat ATK, the Phase-C-deferred buffs flagged in the Attack/Charge Speed ADR above), Ada Wong (Covert Support's bursted-B3 subset buff). Closes gap #3 in `docs/engine-gaps.md`.

## Ada Wong: Special Modification's charge-speed half folded into a net charge-damage bonus (magazine-boundary trap)
- Date: 2026-07-16
- Context: Ada Wong's burst grants Special Modification for 1 round: "Charge Speed ▼300% + Charge Damage ▲1500%." Fienn's ruling was to model BOTH halves literally — "Charge Speed ▼X%" = charge time × (1 + X/100), i.e. ×4 slower, wired as `charge_speed_percent = -0.75` under the existing divide-by-(1+speed) formula. Implementation then hit the documented magazine-boundary trap (see `docs/insights.md`, "charge_speed_percent granularity"): charge speed is only evaluated once per magazine start, and a 1-round Effect granted mid-magazine covers no magazine boundary — so the ×4 slowdown NEVER actually lands, verified by `test_one_round_charge_speed_grant_is_inert_mid_magazine`. Modeling the pair literally would silently credit the full +1500% Charge Damage without ever paying its ×4 time cost.
- Decision: Apply Fienn's documented fallback instead of the raw pair: a net 1-round `charge_damage_bonus` of `15/4 − 1 = +2.75` (the nominal +1500% bonus divided by the ×4 time cost it was supposed to cost, minus the round's own baseline of 1). No `charge_speed_percent` effect is granted at all — the slowdown is folded entirely into a smaller, "paid for" damage bonus on that one round instead.
- Why: A literal implementation of the two-buff pair, given the engine's existing per-magazine granularity, would be strictly better than the real skill (all of the damage bonus, none of the time cost) — an unintended overstatement caused by an engine limitation, not a deliberate simplification. Folding the time cost into the damage number keeps the net DPS effect honest without requiring the engine to support sub-magazine charge-speed windows (out of scope — see the general granularity insight).
- Consequences: See `backend/app/skill_rules/ada_wong.py`'s module docstring and `build_ada_wong_rules` for the derivation. This is the second known instance of the magazine-boundary charge-speed trap (the first being the general insight logged when Attack/Charge Speed were first built) — any future unit whose kit pairs a short-lived charge-speed grant with a charge-damage grant should be checked against this same trap before encoding literally.

## Ada Wong: Flash Grenade's "activation time condition ▼1 sec" only speeds up FB windows opened by her OWN burst
- Date: 2026-07-16
- Context: Ada Wong's burst enhancement text reads "activation time condition ▼1 sec for 10 sec" on Flash Grenade, a periodic during-Full-Burst nuke normally ticking every 2s.
- Decision (Fienn ruling): interpreted as — Full Burst windows whose start falls inside `[her own burst time, +10s)` tick at 1s instead of 2s. Implemented via `periodic_nukes`' `own_burst_interval` field, `(1.0, 10.0)`, using a `<=` (inclusive) comparison for the window start, since her own burst and the Full Burst window's start share the same instant (the same inclusive-start convention already established for `full_burst_bonus_eligible`'s window check).
- Why: The engine already has `full_burst_windows` as the anchor for "during Full Burst" periodic nukes; scoping the faster tick to windows opened specifically by HER burst (not every FB window in the fight) matches the "for 10 sec" duration qualifier in the raw text, which would be meaningless if the faster tick applied to every window regardless of timing.
- Consequences: See `ada_wong.py`. Reuses the inclusive-start convention already documented for `full_burst_bonus_eligible` (`docs/insights.md`) rather than introducing a new boundary rule.

## Jill Valentine: Acid Ammo's refreshing DoT collapses to a continuous whole-fight sustained nuke
- Date: 2026-07-16
- Context: Jill's Acid Ammo applies a 192%/1s DoT (30s duration) on the first bullet of every magazine (battle start + every reload-to-max). Her reload cadence (~6s) is far shorter than the DoT's 30s duration, so applications overlap constantly.
- Decision (Fienn ruling): overlapping applications of the same DoT REFRESH (the general NIKKE convention for same-source DoTs), not stack. Given the ~6s-cadence-vs-30s-duration ratio, the refreshed DoT never has a gap — it's continuously active from shortly after battle start through fight end. Modeled directly as a steady-state simplification: a plain whole-fight periodic sustained nuke (NOT gated `during_full_burst`), rather than literally simulating each reload's refresh-and-truncate.
- Why: Same "steady-state approximation for ramping/refreshing buffs" pattern already used elsewhere (see `docs/insights.md`) — valid here specifically because the fill cadence (6s) is far faster than the effect's own decay window (30s), so the real ramp-up is negligible against a 180s fight and the refresh never actually lapses.
- Consequences: See `backend/app/skill_rules/jill_valentine.py`'s `build_acid_ammo_periodic_nuke`. If a future unit has a similar refreshing DoT but with a cadence closer to (or slower than) its duration, this steady-state shortcut would NOT apply — check the ratio before reusing this pattern, per the existing steady-state insight's caveat.

## Ark Ranger Black: per-unit floor/ceiling bracket flag instead of a general Pattern B time-decay gauge primitive
- Date: 2026-07-16
- Context: Ark Ranger Black's damage is almost entirely gated on a Transformation state driven by a battery gauge (1%/0.2s self-depletion, transform at 100%). Her battery fills (a) +50% per enemy part destroyed and (b) via her burst (Emergency Charge: set to 100% → transform → 50%). The engine has no enemy-part concept, and part-destruction timing is per-player/per-boss — unknowable. The roadmap's "gap #2 Pattern B" bucket (see "Named-resource / capped-stack-counter primitive (gap #2 Pattern A only)" above) turned out to be several unrelated per-unit mechanics (Ark Ranger's decaying gauge, Mihara's chains, red-hood's charge speed), not one primitive.
- Decision: Bracket her output with a new boss-profile flag `BossProfile.part_destructible` (threaded via `simulate_raid` → `SquadContext`, mirroring `boss_element`). `False` = **floor**: transformation is burst-driven only, window `[burst, burst+D]` with D derived from skill values (`50 / (1%/0.2s) = 10s`); `True` = **ceiling**: permanent transformation from battle start (max-potential upper bound; the brief pre-100% ramp is deliberately ignored — Fienn approved). Buffs branch via a `boss_part_destructible()` condition; DoT specs branch via an optional `requires_part_destructible` field filtered in the `resource_scaled_nukes`/`periodic_nukes` loops (absent = always fires, fully backward compatible). The flag is a per-boss input the player supplies each solo-raid season, exactly like element/core_hittable/enemy_def — no season-boss database.
- Alternatives considered & rejected: (1) a general time-decay gauge + threshold-transformation state-machine primitive (only Ark Ranger consumes it — YAGNI; Fienn de-scoped to this one unit); (2) a simplified "fixed N seconds transformed per burst" approximation (opaque, wouldn't compose if part-destruction lands later); (3) deferring the unit entirely (Fienn wanted Pattern B attempted).
- Why: the unknowable part-destruction schedule becomes an explicit, player-visible modeling choice instead of an invented fill schedule; running both flag values brackets her true output (floor ≈ 1067.8M vs ceiling ≈ 1156.0M in the e2e test deck); avoids overestimation from a guessed schedule.
- Consequences: part-destruction battery fill remains deferred (the reason the flag exists); the bracket is a per-unit workaround — gap #2 Pattern B is NOT generally solved, and future gauge units (e.g. Mihara) need their own analysis. One open question: the ceiling Collider goes through `periodic_nukes`, whose loop has no `full_burst_bonus_eligible` parameter, so its ticks inside Full Burst windows can't receive the FB bonus until that loop gains the parameter (future engine extension). Related: design spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-16-ark-ranger-black-transformation-design.md`; the same session also generalized the FB-bonus rule (repeating-tick DoTs get it; "sequentially"-type cast-time multi-hit bursts structurally can't — recorded in the encoding skill's `references/special-mechanics.md`, Fienn 2026-07-16).

## Gap #5: boss_element context, enemy_def_percent wiring, and Full Burst Bonus timing for a Burst-2 "additional damage" bullet
- Date: 2026-07-16
- Context: Encoding Brid: Silent Track's Wind debuffs, Helm: Aquamarine's Aegis Cannon Overload, and Marciana: Marine Study surfaced three separate gap-#5 issues. (1) Several units' debuffs/bullets only apply "if the enemy is X Code" (Brid's Silent Track, Helm's Aquamarine, Marciana's Marine Study), but `SkillRule` actions had no access to the boss's element — only `raid_simulator` (via `BossProfile`) knew it. (2) Marciana's burst applies "High-Risk Target: DEF ▼ 10.56%" to Electric enemies; the damage formula already accepted `enemy_def_percent` but `raid_simulator` never read it from the registry, so it was inert for all units, not just her. (3) Helm's Aegis Cannon Overload has an Electric-only "as additional damage" bullet (Full-Burst-Bonus-eligible by Fienn's text-phrase rule), but Helm is Burst 2, so the engine fires her burst at `tier3_time - gap` — strictly BEFORE `full_burst_windows`' start (which equals the tier-3 burst time) — so an eligibility check at her own-burst time can never pass.
- Decision: (1) Thread the boss's element onto `SquadContext` (`SquadContext.boss_element`, injected by `raid_simulator` from `BossProfile.element`) and add a `boss_is_element(element)` condition helper (`squad_engine.py`), mirroring the existing `ally_bursted`/`deck_contains` condition pattern. Also added an optional `condition` param to the `buff_rule`/`refreshing_buff_rule`/`instant_nuke_pulse_rule` helpers so a gated bullet reuses the same builder as an ungated one, rather than gating inside each action (rejected — duplicates the check across modules). (2) Wire `enemy_def_percent` as a one-line read in `raid_simulator._damage_instance` (`enemy_def_percent=registry.total_for("enemy_def_percent", target, time)`), emitted as a squad-scope enemy debuff exactly like `damage_taken_up`. No DEF=0 floor clamp added (no unit reaches it — boss DEF 31784 × (1 − 0.1056) stays positive); note the reference's "defense cannot drop below 0 through DEF% debuffs" as a future clamp if a bigger debuff appears. (3) Helm's "as additional damage" bullet is modeled fired on her own burst WITHOUT the Full Burst bonus (rather than marking it eligible, which would be inert dead code, or shifting its fire time to `full_burst_start`) — Fienn's call. It understates that one bullet by ~50% but keeps the "fires at cast time" model literal; this is the edge case the codebase previously flagged ("watch for this if a future unit's 'as additional damage' nuke fires exactly at burst time"). Contrast: Marciana (Burst 3) fires her "additional damage" Flagged Target nuke AT `full_burst_start`, so it DOES land in the window and correctly gets the bonus.
- Why: boss_element gating mirrors the existing condition-helper pattern rather than inventing a new mechanism; `enemy_def_percent` was already fully specified by the damage formula and just needed one read site; Helm's fire-time model was chosen over both alternatives because marking her ineligible-in-practice bullet "eligible" is dead code that misrepresents the model, while moving her fire time to `full_burst_start` would be a bigger, unit-specific hack for one bullet.
- Consequences: `boss_element` is already threaded end-to-end (`deck_search` passes `BossProfile.element`), so these debuffs matter in real recommendations. `enemy_def_percent` now moves damage for every attacker in every deck, not just Marciana's — a small (~15 loc core) but broad-reaching wire. Solo-raid global assumptions adopted while encoding Marciana, applicable to all encodings going forward: Rapture count is always 1 (the boss) — "3 or fewer Raptures" conditions are always met, "6 or more Raptures" bullets never fire; boss DEF = 31784 (the `enemy_def` value for solo-raid sims — `BossProfile.enemy_def` defaults to 0.0, so callers must set this); "Flagged Target" / "highest Max HP enemy" = the boss (single/highest-HP enemy in a raid). See `docs/insights.md` ("Full Burst Bonus vs burst tier timing gotcha") for the general FB-timing rule this surfaced.

## Attack Speed / Charge Speed are now modeled (Phase S), reversing the prior "do not build" decision
- Date: 2026-07-16
- Context: Attack Speed and Charge Speed were previously documented as NOT modeled — filed in `docs/engine-gaps.md`'s "만들지 않는 것" (do-not-build) section and in the `nikke-skill-encoding` skill's "Stats the engine does NOT consume" list — on the rationale that they were inert (unwired, no damage-formula consumer).
- Decision (reversal, per Fienn): that rationale was wrong. In a fixed 180s fight, a shorter shot interval means MORE shots fired before the fight ends, so Attack Speed and Charge Speed DO increase total damage — "inert" was an artifact of the engine not wiring them yet, not a conceptual irrelevance to a fixed-duration DPS race. Fienn requested them built because they are the MAIN skill effect of both Tove and Dorothy: Serendipity — encoding those two units without them would misrepresent their entire kit as buff-only fluff.
- What was built (Phase S, commits `d5ff530`/`f26d6a1`/`b127f46`): `attack_speed_percent` (magazine weapons: `shot_interval = 1/(rate_of_fire*(1+speed))`) and `charge_speed_percent` (charge weapons: `effective_charge = charge_time/(1+speed))`), threaded through `attack_rate.py`'s `generate_shot_times`/`generate_magazine_shot_times`/`generate_charge_shot_times` AND the last-bullet marker functions (`last_bullet_shot_times`/`magazine_last_bullet_times`/`charge_last_bullet_times`) via the same `_at(t)` callable pattern already used for `max_ammo_percent_at`/`reload_speed_percent_at`, evaluated once per magazine boundary — a deliberate approximation matching the existing granularity of those other live-recomputed buffs, not a live per-shot recompute. Wired into `raid_simulator.py` via `registry.total_for`. Default `_zero` leaves all pre-existing units' shot timelines byte-identical (regression-safe, verified against all 53 previously-encoded units). Raid-level interaction tests (`test_raid_simulator.py`) confirm Attack Speed increases normal-attack shot count/damage, correctly ripples into per-shot "every N" trigger firing counts (since those count real shots), and Charge Speed increases charged-shot count.
- Why: The prior "inert, don't build" call conflated "not yet wired" with "doesn't matter" — a shorter interval in a fixed-duration simulation is mathematically equivalent to a damage multiplier, just expressed as extra shots rather than extra per-shot percent. Once two units' headline kits depended on it, the honest choice was to build the real mechanism rather than encode them thinly around a gap.
- Consequences: First consumer: Dorothy: Serendipity's False Salvation burst (self Attack Speed +65% for 15s) — clean self-scope, fully modeled. Tove's Attack Speed buff (+42.24%) and her ATK-variant buff (+24.21% of caster ATK, mirroring the same stack count) are scoped to "shotgun allies only" (a weapon-type scope), which this engine still can't express (self/squad/element scopes only) — modeling them correctly needs Phase C's weapon-type scope (gap #3 in `docs/engine-gaps.md`). A squad-wide approximation was considered and REJECTED: over-applying Attack Speed to non-shotgun allies would distort the deck search by crediting DPS to units that can't actually benefit. Both of Tove's buffs are DEFERRED to Phase C together (see `tove.py`'s module docstring and gap #3's consumer list). Hit Rate and Burst Gauge fill speed remain genuinely unconsumed (no damage-formula or timing mechanism reads them) and stay on the do-not-build list — this reversal applies to Attack/Charge Speed only, not the whole former "do not build" bucket. The `nikke-skill-encoding` skill's "Stats the engine does NOT consume" reference still lists Attack/Charge Speed as of this writing and should be corrected to match on its next update pass (out of this doc's ownership).

## Grave: Overheat permanence corrected by in-game verification, not datamined text
- Date: 2026-07-16
- Context: Grave's Overheat skill has three tiers. The datamined text says Overheat I "ATK +15.48%, Removed upon reloading to max ammunition"; Overheat II (30 normals in Prediction) "ATK +20.66% continuously"; Overheat III (60 normals in Prediction) "Attack Damage +30.8% continuously". A prior encoding pass flagged an ASSUMPTION that "continuously" meant permanent-once-unlocked for II/III, and deferred Overheat I entirely as an unmodeled reload-toggle.
- Decision: Fienn verified in the actual game and corrected both halves. Overheat I is actually PERMANENT once unlocked — the "removed on full reload" text does not recur in practice, so it's now modeled (was previously deferred) as a one-time permanent self ATK grant at the 15th normal attack from battle start (gap #1's `after` mode, `unlock_overheat_i`). Overheat II and III are active ONLY while in Prediction (her own burst's 10s status window) — NOT permanent, contradicting the prior "continuously = permanent" assumption. Modeled via gap #7's `every_during_own_status_window` mode: granted refreshing and bounded to the current Prediction window's end, so each fades at window end and re-earns itself every cycle. II is gated on Overheat I already unlocked; III on Overheat II having been reached.
- Why: Authoritative source hierarchy — Fienn's in-game check overrides both datamined skill text and a prior modeling assumption. "Continuously" in the raw text turned out to mean "for the duration of the status window this tier's condition is scoped to" (Prediction), not "forever," while the OPPOSITE tier (Overheat I, whose text implied removal) turned out to be the actually-permanent one. Whenever an encoding carries a flagged "does 'continuously' mean permanent?" assumption, resolve it against in-game behavior before finalizing, not against the raw text.
- Consequences: See `backend/app/skill_rules/grave.py`'s module docstring and `build_overheat_per_shot_rules` for the corrected implementation. Overheat I moved from deferred to modeled; Overheat II/III's model changed from permanent to window-bounded-refreshing. Any other unit whose encoding notes flag the same "'continuously' unclear, assumed permanent" pattern should be treated as unverified until checked the same way.

## Gap #1 last-bullet re-encoding batch: Julia (base), Helm, Privaty consume the magazine-boundary marker
- Date: 2026-07-12
- Context: With the last-bullet capability built (see "Magazine-boundary 'last bullet' marker..." below), three previously-deferred units were re-encoded against it: Julia (base)'s Crescendo (the original motivating case for the whole investigation), Helm's Frontline Command (previously dead code — see below), and Privaty's LD Assault (a new mechanic shape combining last-bullet with a status-window bonus).
- Decision: (1) Julia's Crescendo modeled as `ResourceSpec(fill=("on_last_bullet",), cap=5, ...)` feeding a self Critical Damage buff (+24.79%/stack, 15s each); her Climax burst's additional hit is a `resource_scaled_nukes` entry GATED (not scaled) at max stacks via `scale_fn=lambda count: 1.0 if count>=5 else 0.0` (the same gate shape a generic engine test — `test_resource_scaled_nuke_threshold_gate_zero_below_cap` — was already written for, anticipating this exact case). Only the additional hit opts into `full_burst_bonus_eligible` (only its own text says "as additional damage"; the base 544.5% hit's text doesn't). (2) Helm's Frontline Command rewired from a dead `fire_trigger("on_last_bullet_hit", ...)` call (never actually dispatched by `simulate_raid`'s real event loop — see the insights.md entry on this) to `per_shot_rules`' `"last_bullet"` mode, using `refreshing_buff_rule` (Prika/Mint precedent — her charge-weapon (SR) magazine can empty faster than the buff's own 5s window, which would otherwise stack instead of refresh). (3) Privaty's LD Assault fires a squad Damage Taken debuff (+10.01%/10s) plus 256.17% additional damage on every last bullet (both `full_burst_bonus_eligible`), PLUS an ADDITIVE 1687% hit stacked on top when the target is currently in her own "Designated Target" status (set by her AK Missile burst, lasting 10s) — confirmed from raw skill text that both fire together, not either/or. Modeled as a live time-window check (`any(bt <= time < bt + designated_duration for bt in context.burst_times[caster_slug])`) evaluated fresh inside the per-shot rule's own action at the shot's own time, since no existing status primitive fits a 10s window against a 40s burst cooldown (too short a fraction of the cycle for `own_burst_fired_this_cycle()`'s per-cycle semantics).
- Why: Julia was the case that originally motivated building the marker; Helm and Privaty were the two other units already flagged in `docs/encoded-nikkes.md` citing this exact gap. Helm's dead-trigger bug was a genuine pre-existing correctness bug independent of this batch — caught only because rewiring naturally required inspecting how the rule actually fired in the real simulator, not just its unit test.
- Consequences: Closes out the three units flagged against gap #1's last-bullet residual in `docs/encoded-nikkes.md`. Privaty's same-instant Damage Taken debuff timing (does a last bullet's own damage see the debuff it just applied? do two debuffs within 10s stack or refresh?) is UNCONFIRMED in-game — both current behaviors are just the engine's existing default Effect semantics (mirroring Soda/Cinderella's confirmed precedent for the self-reference question, unlike Maiden's confirmed-opposite case), not verified facts; flagged in `privaty.py`'s module docstring as an open item, the same way Maiden's self-buff-timing question was flagged before Fienn confirmed it in-game. `test_deck_search.py`'s real-data Privaty fixture needed an `"ld_assault"` sub-dict added (realistic Lv7 values matching the fixture's existing `ak_missile` Lv7 data) once Privaty was registered in `_PER_SHOT_RULE_BUILDERS`, since that map's builders run unconditionally for every deck containing the slug — fixed by completing the fixture's data rather than making the encoding tolerate missing data.

## Magazine-boundary "last bullet" marker mirrors the existing shot-generation function shape (gap #1 closeout)
- Date: 2026-07-12
- Context: Gap #1's last residual variant — a burst/skill trigger that fires specifically on the shot that EMPTIES a magazine, right before reload ("마지막 탄", e.g. Julia (base)'s Crescendo, Helm's last-bullet trigger — both flagged "매거진경계 마커 부재" in `docs/encoded-nikkes.md`) — was unbuilt. Fienn pointed at `test_attack_rate.py::test_rate_of_fire_matches_fienns_60fps_table` and `app/attack_rate.py`'s existing magazine/shot-generation machinery as the base to build on, and flagged [Max Ammo Up] and attack/charge-speed buffs as concerns to account for.
- Decision: Add `magazine_last_bullet_times`/`charge_last_bullet_times`/`last_bullet_shot_times` to `attack_rate.py`, mirroring the EXISTING `generate_magazine_shot_times`/`generate_charge_shot_times`/`generate_shot_times`'s exact 3-tier structure (2 low-level raw-parameter functions + 1 weapon-dispatching wrapper) rather than changing those functions' return shape to carry the marker inline. Each new function re-derives `magazine_size` LIVE from the same `max_ammo_percent_at` callable the existing functions already thread through, so [Max Ammo Up] buffs shift the last-bullet marker automatically. Attack/charge speed were deliberately NOT modeled for this feature. Wired into `raid_simulator.py`'s `per_shot_rules` as a new `"last_bullet"` mode (reusing the existing `(threshold, mode, rules)` tuple shape with `threshold` unused), computed once per unit only when at least one `"last_bullet"` rule is present; the pre-existing inline `max_ammo_percent_at`/`reload_speed_percent_at` lambdas were extracted into named locals so `generate_shot_times` and `last_bullet_shot_times` share identical callables and produce bit-identical magazine boundaries (needed since the check is a float membership test).
- Why: An additive, same-structure-mirroring set of new functions achieves the feature with zero changes to any existing test or call site — the alternative (changing `generate_shot_times`'s return shape to e.g. `list[tuple[time, is_last]]`) would have broken 11 of `test_attack_rate.py`'s 13 existing tests for no real benefit. Attack/charge speed are structurally irrelevant here: neither is wired as a shot-interval modifier anywhere in this engine (shot cadence is fixed, computed once — see the "Stats the engine does NOT consume" list), and even if they were, they'd change shot CADENCE, not magazine CAPACITY, so they couldn't move which round is "last" regardless. Only `max_ammo_percent` determines magazine size.
- Consequences: Closes gap #1 fully (previously "'마지막 탄'만 잔여"). A magazine truncated by `fight_duration` ending mid-magazine (not because it was actually emptied) is correctly NOT marked as a last bullet — handled by construction, since `is_last` checks whether the recorded round's index reaches `magazine_size - 1`, not whether it's the last round appended to the output list (see `test_magazine_last_bullet_times_does_not_mark_a_fight_duration_truncated_shot`). The capability is built and tested but not yet used to re-encode any Nikke — Julia (base)'s Crescendo and Helm's last-bullet trigger (both currently deferred in `docs/encoded-nikkes.md` citing this exact gap) are the two immediately-unblocked candidates, not yet touched.

## full_burst_bonus_eligible generalizes beyond the "as additional damage" text phrase for repeating-tick DoTs
- Date: 2026-07-12
- Context: Fienn tested Mana's burst "Fatal Error!" (a flat, non-resource-scaled repeating DoT via `resource_scaled_nukes`, see the entry below) in-game and found its damage receives the base Full Burst Bonus (+50%), even though its skill text says "as sustained damage" — NOT the exact "as additional damage" phrase the existing text-phrase rule (see "full_burst_bonus wiring is opt-in per damage instance, gated on skill-text phrase" below) keys on. `resource_scaled_nukes` also had no `full_burst_bonus_eligible` wiring at all until now (only `record()`/`dynamic_hit_count_nukes` had it).
- Decision: (1) Add `full_burst_bonus_eligible` support to `resource_scaled_nukes`, mirroring the existing opt-in flag on `record()`/`dynamic_hit_count_nukes`. (2) Set it on Mana's `build_fatal_error_dot` spec. (3) Adopt a refined encoding rule going forward: the "as additional damage" text phrase is Fienn's proxy for a TIME-based fact — damage NOT computed at the cast instant, that happens to land inside a Full Burst window, gets the bonus. For a SINGLE-INSTANT nuke, cast-time vs. delayed is genuinely ambiguous from text alone, so the phrase check remains the right heuristic there. For a REPEATING-TICK DoT (any `resource_scaled_nukes` consumer), every tick after the first is inherently computed at a time strictly after cast "by construction" — so `full_burst_bonus_eligible=True` should be the DEFAULT assumption for repeating-tick damage, independent of whether the unit's own text happens to say "as additional damage," subject to confirming the DoT's duration doesn't obviously extend past the Full Burst window.
- Why: The phrase was always a proxy for timing, not the fundamental mechanism; Mana's case (different wording, same underlying "not cast-time" fact for later ticks) confirms the underlying rule is about WHEN damage is computed relative to cast, not which words the skill text uses. This scopes the existing phrase-based rule rather than reversing it — it's still correct for the case it was designed for (single-instant nukes).
- Consequences: Future single-instant nukes still need the phrase check (unchanged). Future repeating-tick DoTs (`resource_scaled_nukes` consumers) should default to `full_burst_bonus_eligible=True` during encoding rather than requiring the phrase. Retroactively applied the same day: Fienn confirmed (2026-07-12) `guillotine_winter_slayer.py`'s Extermination Hero-Level DoT (an earlier `resource_scaled_nukes` consumer, predates this capability) works on the same mechanism — `full_burst_bonus_eligible=True` added to her spec too.

## Delayed dynamic-hit-count nukes, own-status-window resource fills, and full_burst_bonus wiring (eb4)
- Date: 2026-07-12
- Context: Encoding Asuka Shikinami Langley: Wille's burst needed two mechanics no existing capability covered. First, her burst's second-phase nuke ("Annihilation") doesn't fire at cast time — it fires ~9s later, when her own "Annihilation State" self-status ends — and its hit count scales off "Anti A.T. Field," a stack debuff she applies to the ENEMY (not a caster-owned resource, unlike every prior `dynamic_hit_count_nukes` consumer, e.g. Maiden's MP). Second, Anti A.T. Field itself fills "+1 every 10 shots, but only while Annihilation State is active" — a resource fill window anchored to HER OWN burst-fire time (9s from her own burst), distinct from the already-built `per_shot_every_during_full_burst` (anchored to the squad's global Full Burst window, a different instant). Fienn approved building both as real capabilities via AskUserQuestion rather than approximating.
- Decision: (1) `dynamic_hit_count_nukes` gains an optional `fire_delay` (seconds); the nuke fires and reads the resource's PRE-reset count at `burst_time + delay` instead of exactly `burst_time`. `ResourceSpec.resets` gains a matching `"own_burst_delayed"` trigger (with its own `delay`) so the resource's own reset lands at the same delayed instant as the nuke that reads it, not at burst time. (2) New `ResourceSpec` fill kind `("per_shot_every_during_own_status_window", n, window_duration)` — counts shots toward the "every n" tally only if they fall within `window_duration` seconds of one of the owner's OWN burst-fire times (`context.burst_times`), mirroring `per_shot_every_during_full_burst`'s shape but with a per-owner, not squad-global, window. (3) `full_burst_bonus_eligible` — an opt-in boolean threaded through `record()`/`_damage_instance` and `Pulse` (both the `drain_instant_damage` and per-shot-nuke drain sites) — is the actual WIRING for the "as additional damage" text rule (see the entry below): at damage-computation time it's checked against the existing `full_burst_windows` list at that specific damage instance's own recorded time (`start <= time < end`), and only sets `full_burst_bonus=1.0` if both the opt-in flag and the window check pass.
- Why: A burst nuke firing on a delay (not at cast time) needed its own timing hook rather than forcing every `dynamic_hit_count_nukes` consumer through a zero-delay default; an own-status window needed to reuse the existing "count shots in a window" shape (`per_shot_every_during_full_burst`) rather than inventing a new fill mechanism, just anchored to a different event stream (`burst_times` instead of `full_burst_windows`). `full_burst_bonus_eligible` reuses the exact opt-in-per-instance pattern already established by `extra_charge_bonus`/`extra_flat_atk` on `record()`.
- Consequences: First consumer of all three: Asuka Shikinami Langley: Wille — Anti A.T. Field's stack-fill uses the status-window fill kind; Annihilation's 9s-delayed nuke uses `fire_delay` + `own_burst_delayed`; both of her "as additional damage" nukes (the unconditional 471.86% every-50-shots nuke and the delayed 6.62% Annihilation nuke) opt into `full_burst_bonus_eligible`. Latent edge case worth flagging for a future unit: `full_burst_start` fires at the SAME timestamp as the tier-3 burst that triggers it (not strictly after), and the window check is inclusive-start (`start <= time < end`) — so a hypothetical eligible nuke fired AT cast time with zero delay would still read as "inside" the Full Burst window under this check. Doesn't affect Asuka (her eligible nukes are either delayed 9s or fire on an unrelated per-shot timer), but the next unit with a same-instant, zero-delay "as additional damage" nuke should have this checked explicitly rather than assumed. Anti A.T. Field's own 15.62%-of-ATK per-shot direct-damage bullet (separate from the 471.86% nuke) remains deferred — it needs a Full-Burst/own-status-WINDOW-GATED PER-SHOT TRIGGER to directly fire a nuke, not just a resource fill, the same still-unbuilt shape as Soda's residual co-fired buff (`engine-gaps.md` gap #7) — Asuka is a second, still-unbuilt consumer of that gap.

## resource_scaled_nukes' "resource" field becomes optional (flat repeating DoT)
- Date: 2026-07-12
- Context: Encoding Mana's Fatal Error! (burst DoT: 396% of final ATK per second, 10 ticks one second apart) needed the same repeating-tick machinery (`tick_count`/`tick_interval`) already built for `resource_scaled_nukes` (Guillotine's Hero-Level DoT), but with NO resource scaling at all — a plain flat DoT.
- Decision: Make `"resource"` optional on a `resource_scaled_nukes` spec dict; when absent, `record()`'s `resource_gate` is `None` (no percent-scaling resolution in phase 2 — the flat `base_percent` is used as-is), reusing the same tick loop instead of requiring a fake always-present resource just to get repeating ticks.
- Why: Smallest change that avoids inventing a dummy resource (e.g. a fake capped-at-1 resource that always reads 1) purely to satisfy a previously-mandatory field — the tick-count/tick-interval repeating-DoT shape is the actually-reusable part, not the resource scaling.
- Consequences: First consumer: Mana's Fatal Error!. Any future flat repeating DoT (no resource involved) reuses this directly instead of round-tripping through a fake resource.

## Cinderella: Crystal Wave reclassified out of the Pattern-A resource backlog
- Date: 2026-07-12
- Context: `roadmap.md` and `engine-gaps.md` listed `cinderella-crystal-wave` as a remaining "Pattern A resource unit" in the same bucket as the already-encoded Quency/Soda/Maiden/Modernia/Guillotine (gap #2's named-resource/capped-stack-counter primitive). Data collection (lootandwaifus.com) during eb4 showed her HEADLINE mechanic is actually MG/Snipe weapon-mode switching — a player-chosen state that gates which Full-Burst-damage variant fires — not a resource-primitive unit. Her actual named resource (ammo → Burst Gauge fill, multi-source including allies) doesn't even touch damage, since Burst-Gauge-fill-speed is on the "engine doesn't consume" list (gauge charge time is a fixed simulation input).
- Decision: Fienn's call (via AskUserQuestion, this session): drop Cinderella: Crystal Wave from eb4 with no replacement, and reclassify her in the backlog docs into the weapon-transform/state-machine category (alongside snow-white, snow-white-heavy-arms, maxwell) rather than Pattern A.
- Why: Her real encoding blocker is a different, unbuilt capability (weapon-mode switching that gates burst-damage variants) — leaving her filed under Pattern A would misdirect future ROI prioritization toward a gap that doesn't actually unblock her.
- Consequences: The Pattern-A resource backlog is smaller than the roadmap previously stated (Cinderella: Crystal Wave removed, no replacement added to Pattern A). The weapon-transform/state-machine backlog gains a member. This docs-keeper pass records the decision here; `docs/roadmap.md` and `docs/engine-gaps.md`'s actual backlog listings are maintained separately and should be updated to match on their next pass.

## full_burst_bonus wiring is opt-in per damage instance, gated on skill-text phrase
- Date: 2026-07-12
- Context: `full_burst_bonus` (+50% Major Modifier) has been unwired in `raid_simulator.py` since the damage formula was built — no call site reads it, so it contributes 0 for all 45 encoded Nikkes today. An earlier theory (Maiden: Ice Rose's Diamond Dust getting it via a ~1s cast-to-damage delay pushing its damage into the Full Burst window) was investigated, written up in `engine-gaps.md`, and then fully reverted via `git revert` after in-game testing showed Diamond Dust's damage is actually computed AT CAST TIME with no delay (see `docs/insights.md`, the retracted-delay-theory bullet under "Effects / stats"). That left "when does a burst nuke get `full_burst_bonus`" unresolved and blocked pending per-unit verification.
- Decision: Fienn gave a general, text-driven rule instead of a per-unit verification process: a burst skill's damage instance receives `full_burst_bonus` if and only if its official skill description contains the phrase "as additional damage"; absent that phrase, the damage is computed from cast-time effects only. Wire `full_burst_bonus` **opt-in per damage instance** — on the specific `record()`/`_damage_instance` call for a unit whose skill text contains that phrase — following the same precedent already set by `extra_charge_bonus`/`extra_flat_atk` on `record()`, not as a blanket engine-wide change to how `full_burst_bonus` is read.
